- thread: "which Israel's law gave to another tribe" reworded to what Heb. 7:14 says (the law gave no priesthood to the king's tribe); setting: "nearest companion" cut.
- christ: "each thing the psalm says of its king" narrowed to the throne and the priesthood; cut the paragraph on D&C 76:56–57 (own connection; now a pointer in `explore`) and the Acts 2:33 and Heb. 10:12 repetitions of the notes.
- explore: cut the Zech. 6 item and the item repeating 1 Cor. 15 and D&C 49:6 (already in the note and `parallels`); the claim tying D&C 84 to verse 4 cut; the pointer to the Religious Studies Center study reworded to what its paragraph 42 says, with the row's quote replaced.
- 110:1 and 110:4 overlap with Scripture Helps (capitals and Adonai; the ideal priest-king and D&C 107:3) is left: the brief makes this psalm the place LORD and Lord are explained in full, and both notes go beyond the help (the Masoretic pointing, the Greek, Uzziah, JST Heb. 7:3).

**Chapter 137**

- 137 `christ`: cut the second paragraph (Luke 19:41, 44; 23:34) as the writer's own typology; reworded 'He claims it in the same words in every age' to Mormon hearing the voice of the Lord (Morm. 3:14-15), since the wording differs between Deut. 32:35, Rom. 12:19 and Morm. 3:15.
- 137 `explore` and `parallels`: cut the Revelation 18:6, 18:22, 14:1-3 and D&C 133:14 item and the Rev. 18:6 parallel; the Church's footnote at Rev. 18:6 does not cite Psalm 137 and no source read joins them. Kept the Jer. 29:7 item as a bare pointer, without 'the same exiles' or the instruction to read it beside the last verses.
- 137:8 cursing psalms (finding 1): the Scripture Helps sentence is now given as what it is, a statement about the 'perfect hatred' of Ps. 139:22, called a case of the same kind, as the brief directs.
- 137:8 cursing psalms (finding 2): added the handbook's qualification that gloating or personal vindictiveness is to be condemned but does not pass into action, with two new ledger rows from the Student Manual.
- 137:8 cursing psalms (finding 3): removed the claim that the psalm 'uses the words of' Jeremiah's sentence or asks for what the Lord 'had said'; the note now says the Lord says the same of Babylon in Jeremiah. I confirmed with hebrew.mjs that Jer. 50:29 and 51:24 also have the verb shillem (H7999) and added rows, so the checker's remark that they use other words was wrong.
- 137:9 (finding 4): 'people who had watched Babylon's army kill their own young' became 'the nation whose army had shown Jerusalem no pity', since 2 Chr. 36:17 names no infants and the psalm is undated; cut 'The verse names the worst thing the singers knew'; 'The psalm did not invent the image' became 'Isaiah says that this would come on Babylon', with the Medes now quoted from Isa. 13:17.
- 137:8 participle note (finding 5): cut 'Jeremiah says both in one line' and 'Babylon devastated'; Jer. 51:56 is cited only for using the verb of Babylon's ruin. I also took Delitzsch out of this note (four rows removed) so the commentary stays in two of eight notes; the note now rests on the Hebrew form, BDB and the NRSVUE, and the point that verse 7 still awaits the reckoning is made from the psalm's own words.
- 137:7 (finding 6): retitled 'What Edom said on the day of Jerusalem'; 'the Lord did remember it' became 'would repay it'; Edom as Esau's nation now rests on Gen. 36:1; the unsourced gloss on gemul was cut; Obadiah is said to describe 'such a day' and is not dated to 586 B.C.
- 137:5 (findings 7 and 8): cut what the King James translators 'judged', keeping only that supplying the art of playing is an old way of completing the line; the NRSVUE is quoted without saying it follows the reflexive reading; the third paragraph (hand and tongue as the musician's, the oath as the answer to the captors) was cut to one sentence on the matching penalty of verse 6.
- 137:4: the link from 'the LORD's song' to the temple music of Chronicles is Delitzsch's, so he is now in the note's sources with a row; cut 'the name of the one it was sung to' and 'the people asking for it were the people who had burned it'.
- 137:1 and 137:3: cut 'did what this verse describes' of Ezekiel, 'Babylonia was a land of rivers and canals', 'the common Hebrew word for joy', and the closing lines 'a psalm about remembering Zion is itself a memory of Babylon' and 'The captors get no mirth because...'.
- `setting` and `thread`: 'lament of the people' and 'a lament usually has a confession of trust and a vow of praise' now rest on KnoWhy #438 (two rows); Lamentations mourning the same fall is quoted from Lam. 1:3; the thread's closing line 'could not strike back... hand to the LORD the memory they could not act on' was cut to what the psalm does.

**Chapter 150**

- 13:1: cut the Ps. 6:3 sentence. It had no ledger row, and I believe from memory that its Hebrew 'how long' differs from this psalm's, which I did not verify; the note opens on 'one Hebrew question'.
- 13:1 hidden face (finding 6): 'the phrase is the language of His warning to Israel' became 'the same phrase stands in His warning'; cut 'uses those words for his own case'. Deut. 31:17 stays because the lexicon and Larsen both set it beside this verse.
- 13:1 hidden face: cut the Ps. 10:11 paragraph (boast against complaint) and its ledger row, and the pointer to verse 3; no source makes either link.
- 13:2 counsel (finding 7): cut 'he is his own and only adviser, and the plans are many'. Delitzsch's reading was not brought in, since it would put him in a third note.
- 13:2 Joseph Smith (finding 8): cut the pairing of 'a small moment' with 'how long' and of 'foes' with the enemy question, and the closing paragraph ('The answer gives no date'). The note now quotes D&C 121:7-8 as the reply. It stays although footnote 1b gives D&C 121:1, because it goes on to the answer.
- 13:3 (finding 9): cut 'Read so, verse 3 asks for the reverse of verse 1'; Delitzsch ties the light to God's countenance but not to the hidden face. 'Because he believes he is dying' became 'lest he die'; 'The death he fears is real' became 'The sleep he fears is death itself'.
- 13:4 (finding 10): 'the Psalms nearly always use with a not' became 'Hebrew poetry mostly uses with a not', the lexicon's wording. Cut the Ps. 10:6 sentence on the wicked man borrowing the confidence.
- 13:5 (finding 11): cut 'while the question was still open'. The contrast with Ps. 22:21 stays; the brief itself pairs the two psalms.
- 13:6 (finding 12): 'Another psalm uses it after a rescue' became 'Another psalm has the same words'; Psalm 116 was not read.
- christ (finding 13): cut 'His face can be asked to shine again' and 'His hesed holds while He is silent'. The paragraph now says what the speaker says: he trusts His hesed, salvation is His, He deals bountifully. 'The word on which Psalm 3 turns' became 'the word of Psalm 3's last verse'.
- christ and explore (overlap): D&C 121:7 is quoted once, in the note on verse 2; christ points there, and the explore bullet 'Read D&C 121:1-10 as question and reply' is cut.
- explore (finding 14): 'asked by the whole people' in Ps. 79:5 and 89:46 became 'The same pairing'; no source was read, and Ps. 89:47 is in the singular.
- explore (finding 15): the Ps. 22:1 bullet with the turn at verse 21 left as is; it points to a finished chapter and follows the brief.
- Limits: Delitzsch stays in 2 of 8 notes (13:3, 13:6), each as one reading, plus setting and thread. Larsen is in 3 notes but is the main idea of only the Liberty Jail note. No Student Manual.

**Chapter 14**

- Setting: cut 'the one who led the singing' as a gloss on the chief Musician (no row); the pointer to Ps. 8:1 carries it.
- Thread: cut 'answered with a count of those who do good, and it comes to none' (the writer's reading); the four uses of ein stay as an observation from the Hebrew.
- Thread: 'my people' of verse 4 being the generation of the righteous is now 'on one reading' (Delitzsch's), not fact; 'set over against the corrupt mass' cut.
- 14:1 (fool): cut the join of verse 1's fool to verse 4's devourers ('known by how he treats people') and the inference that 'the psalm does not need him to say it aloud'.
- 14:1 (JST): 'The same study suggests' reworded so the source is not announced.
- 14:2: the Flood and Babel echoes are now given as heard by one reading, with the Hebrew showing shared words but not intent; cut 'The fool decided in his heart; the LORD looks before He speaks' and the gloss that 'seek God' defines wisdom as 'the fool's opposite'.
- 14:3 (First Vision): 'Within months' hedged to 'Probably within months'; 'concludes' softened to match the article's conditional wording; JS-H 1:19 is now set beside verse 1 by the article rather than stated as the Lord using verse 1's word; 'scholars' readings' reworded.
- 14:3 (First Vision): the Church's heading is now given in full (loss of truth and the establishment of Zion) instead of 'says only'.
- 14:3 (Paul): 'warns of a day when' replaced by Moroni's own condition quoted from Moro. 10:24; cut 'both times says where the exception lies' and the rhetorical question; 'for five more verses' became 'through verse 18'; 'at full strength' became 'applies to all'.
- 14:3 (Paul): D&C 33:4 stays, since Wright and Bradley's note says the revelation quotes Psalm 14:3; a note of the article is the article.
- 14:4: Micah 3:3 is now the thought 'carried further' on the same older reading, not 'a butcher's scene'; cut the closing paragraph that restated the verse and repeated the verse 1-verse 4 join.
- 14:5: opening no longer calls the verse 'the psalm's turn' (the thread says that); cut "'There' points at the scene without saying when" (a Hebrew claim with no row) and 'a stronger verb'.
- 14:6: 'takes it as an attempt' became 'renders it'; cut 'they may thwart or mock it, and he is sheltered all the same'. The Joseph Smith Translation paragraph stays because the reading of 'the counsel of the poor' as a title for God goes beyond the footnote.
- 14:7 (captivity): 'has been used to question the title's ascription to David' became 'might seem to date the psalm there' (the row shows only a conditional); 'who was never carried anywhere' became 'Job's restoration'. The note keeps the brief's rule: the title is neither argued nor questioned beyond the one older reading, labelled as such.
- 14:7 (Zion): cut the Greek rendering of the wish (a curiosity, with an unsupported 'keeps the wish as a question') along with its ledger row and lxx-rahlfs in that note's sources; cut the closing paragraph claiming the last line 'stands as it was' (no row compares it); 'the Saints hoped to build' became 'then beginning'.
- christ: 'the dark half of his witness of Christ' and 'joins the two in one voice' reworded to plain statements of what Romans 3 and the 1832 history say.
- explore: 'line by line' became 'compares' (the row shows only that the article compares them). parallels: Moro. 10:25 line made grammatical.
- Left as is: three notes on the Joseph Smith Translation (14:1, 14:3, 14:7), since each goes beyond footnote 1a; Delitzsch in 3 of 10 notes, each labelled a reading; no note on the title, which has only 'To the chief Musician, A Psalm of David'.

**Chapter 15**

- Setting: 'Psalm 24 is the other psalm of this kind' became 'another psalm of this kind'; the source says 'another', and a second source lists more such psalms.
- 15:1 (guest): cut 'Psalm 61 turns the question into a wish' (the writer's join); Ps. 61:4 now stands as the same verb of the same tent, and 'dwelling-place of righteous' is said of both psalms (15 and 61), not of Psalm 5, which the lexicon does not list there.
- 15:1 (Joseph Smith Translation): 'it was made when' Zion was prominent became 'The work on the Psalms has been dated approximately to January and February 1833', as LeFevre's dating rather than fact, with a new ledger row I saw on his page.

- 15:4 (reprobate): cut 'It is a minority reading'; the commentary says only that the line 'is mostly translated' as the King James has it, and the note now says only that.
- 15:4 (oath): the sentence no longer says the commentary judged the Greek translation by name; it now says a rendering 'to his neighbour' takes the word for hurt as the word for neighbour, which is what the commentary discusses.
- 15:5 (never be moved): cut the paragraph that Ps. 125:1 uses the same verb of Mount Zion, with its parallels entry and three ledger rows. No source applies it to this verse, as with the same-verb remark cut from Psalm 1. Ps. 16:8 stays, since the lexicon lists it beside 15:5 in the same sense.
- 15:5 (ten requirements): kept the one-sentence temple recommend comparison, since two of the brief's named sources make it and it draws no line to the endowment; reworded so no study is announced, with the 'is a valid connection' hedge kept.
- christ: 'hears the psalm's answer in the Savior's words' became 'sets the psalm's answer beside the Savior's saying'. The commentary paraphrases Matt. 7:21 without citing it; the saying is unmistakable, so the reference stays.
- christ: left resting on the Church's footnote cross-references at verse 1 (D&C 76:62; Moses 6:57). No scripture or Church source reads the psalm of Christ, and the brief asks for a short section with no typology in that case.
- explore: cut 'and answers more briefly' of Ps. 24:3-6 (no row), and cut the bullet 'The law behind verse 5', which repeated the references of the note on 15:5 and of parallels.
- Delitzsch stays in 3 of 10 notes (15:4 twice, 15:5), each labelled an older reading; that is the brief's limit of a third.
- Setting: the Ps. 14:7 'sequel' reading stays, labelled one reading. Thread: 'three broad marks' stays, since it is quoted from the commentary.

**Chapter 16**

- 16:10 Holy One: cut the sentence that the Hebrew as written has a plural. The lexicon calls the written form plural, but the Leningrad text prints the singular and Delitzsch says the best manuscripts read the singular; the reader's question does not need the variant. Ledger row dropped.
- 16:10 Holy One: the chapter heading's "His Holy One (the Messiah)" is no longer quoted or announced; the note now says both apostles apply the word to the Messiah and points to the next note.
- 16:10 Sheol: cut "pictures a soul that goes there and does not stay" (the writer's inference; Delitzsch leans the other way) and "That is the sense Peter draws out" (no source joins the Hebrew verb to Peter).
- 16:10 Sheol: cut the restatement of Sheol (Ps. 6:5 explains it), the repeated Acts 2:31 quotation, and the quoted Scripture Helps and heading phrases; the note paraphrases and points to the corruption note and Ps. 51:14. Two ledger rows dropped.
- 16:4: "The second refusal has the law behind it" became "has a counterpart in the law", since the source gives only a point of contact with Ex. 23:13. Also cut "A drink offering was poured", which had no row.
- 16:5: cut the sentence calling all five words (portion, inheritance, lot, lines, heritage) terms for a family's holding; only "lines" and "portion/lot" had rows.
- 16:11: "it belongs to the temple" cut. The two Skinner statements are now given separately, because he cites this verse for joy in the Lord's presence, not as a temple-face psalm.
- 16:11: cut the closing paragraph, which repeated the thread and printed the Larsen clause a second time; Larsen removed from the note's sources and its row dropped.
- christ: cut "What the speaker of the psalm expected because the LORD was his portion, God did for His Son" (the writer's link). The apostles' argument is paraphrased with references instead of quoted a third time.
- christ: Larsen's sentence is kept once, worded as an application that has been made to all who die, not as plain fact and not as "our bodies".
- explore and parallels: cut "A fulness of joy" (D&C 138:17, 93:33-34) from both, since no source joins those verses to Ps. 16:11. Cut the "David's promise" bullet, which had no row and duplicated the pointer to Ps. 51:14 in the Sheol note. Two ledger rows dropped.
- thread: cut "Where an Israelite would point to a field, he points to God" (the writer's gloss) and reworded the Delitzsch row's claim so it no longer says verse 10 explains the opening plea.
- tagline: "his trust reaches past death" became "God will not abandon him to death", which is as far as verse 10 goes.
- 16:8: "a poetic name for the self" became the lexicon's own words ("the seat of honour in the inner man").
- 16:2: cut "the speaker claims nothing as his own", fixed an ambiguous "it" about which edition marks the Hebrew uncertain. 16:3: the beside/against framing reduced to the plain order of the verses. 16:7: cut the closing paragraph that repeated the opening sentence.
- Left as is: the setting's "no complaint, no enemy is described, no deliverance is reported" and the thread's hinge at "Therefore" in verse 9. Both are plain observations of the text, and the brief makes the thread the psalm's movement.
- Left as is: the corruption note's first two paragraphs (Peter, then Paul), although the footnote and Scripture Helps give the argument. The brief calls for the New Testament verse to be quoted for Psalm 16, and the note goes on to the Hebrew "pit" against the Greek "corruption".
- Left as is: Delitzsch in 3 of 11 notes, each labelled one reading. After the cuts Larsen is the main idea of no note (one sentence in 16:8, one row in the corruption note, the setting and christ).

**Chapter 17**

- Setting, 17:12: 'one of them is singled out as a lion' became 'pictured in verse 12 as a lion'; no source says the lion is the leader.
- Setting, 17:11: 'Others are in danger with him, since verse 11 says us' left as it is. The King James says 'us' and a ledger row has the commentary's 'persecuted, and others with him'. The written Hebrew has 'me' (ketiv) and the read form 'us' (qere); the chapter does not say so.
- Thread, 17:1-2 and 17:15: the return of the three Hebrew words (right, face, behold) is kept as an observation the Hebrew text shows, with its rows. 'At the beginning God is asked to look at him' was wrong (verse 2's eyes behold 'the things that are equal') and is corrected; 'turned around' is cut.
- Thread, 17:6-9: 'The ground of that request is God's marvellous lovingkindness' became 'He asks God to show...'; verse 6 gives its own ground.
- 17:1 (just cause): 'Sentence does not mean a punishment' became 'is the judgment given in his case', which the Greek row supports; no lexicon row existed for the stronger claim.
- 17:1 (Joseph Smith's revision): cut 'The revision was made by early 1833', which the setting already says.
- 17:3: 'belongs to the same picture [refining]' became 'is an examination too', which is as far as the lexicon row goes; cut the closing 'God has come to examine him'.
- 17:8: 'The image had been used before' became 'is also used', since the psalm is undated. The link of the wings to Deut. 32:11 now rests on the commentary's own sentence, quoted as the same older reading, with a new row.
- 17:8: cut the quotation of Ps. 36:7 and the claim that it 'joins [the wings] to the word of verse 7' (the writer's own join); the verse stays as a reference with Ps. 91:4, both in the lexicon row.
- 17:13: 'supplied by the translators' became 'the Hebrew has no words for which is and which are', which the Hebrew rows show; the footnote says only 'OR by'.
- 17:13: 'Both take the sword and the hand as God's own means of rescue' narrowed to the sword. The Greek of verse 14 has 'enemies of thine hand', and no row quotes the modern translation's verse 14.
- 17:14: 'part of its text has been judged dubious' narrowed to the clause about children, which is all the lexicon says; 'is hard' became 'is read in more than one way'.
- 17:15 (face): 'runs through the book' became 'is a theme of several of them', matching the source's 'several'.
- 17:15 (awake): 'a standard lexicon prefers' the LORD's awaking is reworded to 'it has also been placed under a further sense'. The lexicon's lowercase 'we below 1b' may be the editors or may be an interpreter, and the new wording is true either way. Added the fourth reading (a night of distress) with a new row. Cut 'The ancient Greek is close to that', the writer's judgment and a repeat of the note before.
- 17:15 (awake): cut the closing sentence 'The verse itself states the hope and does not say when it will be met', so the note ends on the Church's footnote and not on a line that sets the other readings beside it as equals.
- christ: cut the identification 'The LORD to whom David prays is the one whose face he hopes to see', 'on a like condition', and the David/Jesus comparison. It now says Matt. 5:8 'has been set beside that line', which is what the row supports.
- explore: cut the song of Moses bullet, which repeated the note on 17:8 and the parallels; 'share this psalm's last line' became 'closing hope'.
- Outside commentary stays in 3 of 10 notes (17:3, 17:8, 17:14), each labelled an older reading, which is the brief's limit of a third.
- 17:1 (Joseph Smith's revision), third paragraph: the comparison with JS-H 1:28 is kept, labelled as the study's own; it is a Religious Studies Center reading of a change the Church's edition does not print.

- 18:0 (when David sang it): cut the link to 2 Sam. 7:1, which no source makes. 'The names for God in verse 2 belong to those years' is now given as the older reading's, quoted from Delitzsch ('the fruits of the affliction... his persecution by Saul'), with a new row.
- 18:2 (names): the opening now states only what the lexicon shows (three of the words are used in the story of the flight from Saul) and points to the title note for the reading that the names come from those years. Delitzsch is not added to this note, which holds him at 5 of 15 notes.
- 18:2 (horn): 'Hannah had sung the same way' reworded to 'Hannah's song has the figure too'; the lexicon groups the verses, so the comparison stays.

- 18:34: 'bent, not broken' is now 'probably bent', and the note says the verb's sense is marked dubious, with a new lexicon row.
- 18:35: retitled 'Gentleness, an uncertain word'; cut the closing paragraph on 'the LORD's own meekness', which stated as the sense a reading the lexicon marks dubious.
- 18:49: cut the identification of the nations with those of verses 43-45; the sentence now uses verse 43's own words. The note stays though footnote 49a gives Rom. 15:9, because it adds the Greek wording and Rom. 15:10-12.
- 18:50: 'the New Testament names the heir' reworded. Gabriel's words (Luke 1:32-33) stay because the Church's footnote at 2 Sam. 7:16 refers to Luke 1:33 and Luke 1:33's footnote refers back; a row was added.
- christ: cut the Rock paragraph (Guide to the Scriptures and Hel. 5:12), a connection no source applies to this psalm, and with it the gs-rock key, row and proposed entry. The Luke 1:69 quotation is printed once, in the horn note; christ points to it.

- Left as is: Delitzsch in 5 of 15 notes, exactly the brief's third, each time as 'one older reading'.

**Chapter 19**

- Setting: 'closer in its language to Proverbs than most psalms are' reworded to the source's 'at times like that of Proverbs (its second half more than its first)'; the Psalm 8 comparison is now part of the older reading's supposition, not stated as fact.

- 19:7 converting: 'open-minded' now carries the lexicon's 'possibly'; 'will be formed by whichever teaches him' cut. The wisdom-hymn paragraph is cut because it repeated the setting, and its 'second half more than the first' row moved to setting. Larsen's Old Testament Minute is now in 3 of 9 notes.
- 19:12: 'a word of the court' and 'a verdict more than a washing' reduced to the lexicon's 'hold innocent, acquit'. 'Drew the same line' is now 'distinguished two kinds of sin'. The Numbers 15 link stays as one reading, and the parallels entry now says so.
- 19:13: rebuilt so the King James 'sins' and the Joseph Smith Translation 'acts' come first and 'presumptuous men' is one reading, not an equal 'open question' with the Church's text on one side. Cut 'keeps that sense' (no source says what the JST means), 'restraint and not pardon' and 'asks to be stopped beforehand'.

The section is now two short paragraphs resting on verses 12-14, as the brief asks for a psalm no scripture reads of Christ.

- Left as is: Delitzsch in 3 of 9 notes (the brief's limit), each labelled a reading; no note on the title, with the setting pointing to Ps. 8:1; the 'six names' count in 19:7, which is checkable in the verses.

- 32:10: cut the closing sentence joining hasid (verse 6) and hesed (verse 10) as one pattern; 'the psalm ends with' became 'the psalm's last promise'; 'covenant word' now rests on the pointer to Ps. 51:1; hasid is 'most often taken' to denote the practice of hesed, as the lexicon hedges.
- Thread: 'Everything after that is said to other people or for their sake' became 'After that the psalm looks beyond David's own case', since verse 7 is said to God.
- christ: first sentence reworded to what Romans 4:24-25 says (Paul's argument, in which he quotes the psalm, ends in Jesus Christ), not that Paul reads the psalm's blessedness as coming through Christ.
- explore and parallels: the Ps. 51:13 tie is now marked as holding on one reading; cut the 'Covering and confessing' bullet (it duplicated note 32:5) and Rom. 4:23-25 from the Paul bullet (it duplicated christ).
- Left as is, finding 1 (verses 7 and 10): the same verb is confirmed in the Hebrew text and the note quotes the King James words; the lexicon's 'dubious' remark on verse 7's object is not carried.
- Left as is, finding 7 (32:8): the lexicon gloss 'advise, counsel' is correct; the commentator's remark on the irregular construction is not needed by the reader's question.
- Left as is, findings 4, 11 and 13: the three-noun distinction is already labelled one older reading; the Selah placement is the text's own; the 'upright in heart' bullet already says 'have been connected'.
- Left as is: John 1:47 and 2 Cor. 5:19 rest on a modern translation's cross-references to verse 2, and are worded as set beside the line, not as the psalm's meaning.

**Chapter 33**

- 33:6: added a ledger row for the Church's footnote 6a (JST John 1; Topical Guide 'Jesus Christ, Creator'), so the link from the verse to John 1 rests on a source. The note stays although the footnotes give the references, because it sets out the psalm's own sense of 'word' first and then what John and D&C 38:3 add.
- 33:6: 'This is the creation account in a single line, where each work begins with speech' became 'The creation account begins the same way'; the row shows one work only.
- 33:15: cut the Greek rendering 'alone' and the older commentary's approval of it, with its reasoning that hearts 'were not from the very first created all at one time'. That reasoning leads into a view of spirits imparted at birth, which is not the Church's teaching, and the reader's question does not need it. Three ledger rows removed; the Septuagint dropped from the note's sources.
- 33:15: the second paragraph is now given as 'one older reading' instead of 'In the words of the same commentary'.
- 33:12: cut Deut. 32:9 (no source applies it to this verse) and 'the covenant at Sinai narrows in the same way'. Ex. 19:5 stays as related scripture ('speaks of the same choosing'), resting on the NRSVUE's cross-reference for the verse.
- 33:12: the Deut. 33:29 link is now 'one older reading' about this part of the psalm, not about the word 'Blessed' alone; 'the rest of the psalm' corrected to 'this part of the song', which is what the commentary says.

- 33:7: retitled 'A heap of waters, or a bottle'. Cut 'the subject is the sea at creation' and the sentence reading the word as an allusion to the exodus and the Jordan crossing; the lexicon only lists the noun's occurrences. The note keeps the word's other uses and the Greek 'bottle' reading.
- 33:11: cut 'Verses 4-9 and verses 10-11 are one reason for praise and not two' and reduced the sentence before it to the repeated verb.
- 33:3: the opening no longer states a rule for all scripture; it says that where scripture gives a reason for a new song the reason is something God has done. Restored the commentary's 'apparently' to the quotation about the psalm's occasion.
- 33:1: 'repeats the last line of Psalm 32' became 'takes up' (the lines share a verb and two groups, and are not identical); cut the closing sentence that said the same thing again.
- 33:2: 'The same instrument goes with the same kind of song once more' became 'The ten-stringed harp is joined to a new song once more', since Ps. 92:3 also names the two words.
- 33:18: cut 'The singers ask to be the people that verse describes', the writer's own reading.
- Setting: 'nineteen give the reasons' became 'the rest gives the reasons and closes in the congregation's own words' (verses 20-22 are the answer, not reasons).
- christ: the John 1:3, D&C 38:3 and Rev. 5:9 quotations were printed in the notes on verses 6 and 3 as well; christ now paraphrases and points to those notes. 'Gives the fuller reason for singing' cut as the writer's own.
- explore: cut 'Horses and the name' (Ps. 20:7 and 147:10-11 are in the notes on 33:17 and 33:18; Prov. 21:31 is the Church's footnote 17a) and 'Moses' last blessing' (repeats the note on 33:12); two ledger rows removed. Two bullets remain.
- Left as is: thread's 'Verse 12 is the hinge' (the brief asks the thread to say where the psalm turns); 33:17 'The psalm does not say the horse is weak' and 33:18's three verbs and 'Behold' (plain observations of the verses); the Guide to the Scriptures row in christ.
- Left as is: Delitzsch in 3 of 10 notes (33:3, 33:12, 33:15), each labelled one older reading. That is under the brief's limit of a third.

- christ: cut the closing line on 3 Ne. 9:20, which repeated the note on verse 18 and the explore bullet.
- thread: 'The word that carries the psalm is all' is now 'The psalm keeps saying all', an observation of the text; hebrew.mjs shows the Hebrew word eight times in the psalm.
- Left as is: the commentary stays in 3 of 9 notes (title, 34:7, 34:20), the brief's limit, each labelled a reading; for that reason it is not cited for the perfect tenses in 34:5 or for 'children' in 34:11.

**Chapter 35**

Cut 'On that reading the prayer he offered for them comes back to him', which no page says; the lexicon's listing of the verse under 'requite into bosom' stays, with the statement that nothing is said of how a prayer is repaid. The bare rendering is now attributed to the modern translation's margin.
- 35:1: cut 'he asks the LORD to be both his champion and his advocate' and 'something no weapon gives' (the writer's synthesis). Removed the Delitzsch clause that repeated the setting, with its row; 1 Sam. 24:15 stays with the lexicon's sense there (taking his part).
- thread: 'What ties the three together is who gets to speak' is now an observation that all three parts quote speech, with the verses. - 35:22: cut 'In verse 17 God's looking on was the grievance. In verse 22 it is the evidence: the Judge was a witness' (an argument built on the shared verb, the writer's). The note keeps the verb in verses 17, 21 and 22 and what verses 22-23 ask. Kind changed from structure to words, since it is a repeated word and not a structure that carries the message.

- 40:6 (ears): cut the paragraph on the pierced ear of Ex. 21:6 and its ledger row. It was a reading raised only to be set aside, and its wording went beyond the commentary's ground (the plural 'ears').
- 40:6 (ears): cut the Isaiah 50:5 sentence ('nearly the same thing'), which no source read makes. The explore bullet and the parallel stay as links to the site's own note on Isa. 50:5, which already points to this verse.
- 40:6 (Hebrews): 'Hebrews gives only Lo, I come ... to do thy will' corrected to 'ends at to do thy will, O God', since Heb. 10:7 keeps the 'volume of the book' clause. 'Builds on two words' became 'takes up two of the words it has quoted'; 'argues from the Greek as it stood' became 'quotes the psalm as the Greek has it'.
- 40:7: 'it depends on the speaker being the king' became 'it has the king most in view', matching the source's 'as a man, and more especially as the king'.
- 40:9: cut 'the ear has become a mouth' and the Luke 4:18 sentence with its row; no source applies Luke 4 to this verse. The shared verb with Isa. 52:7 and 61:1 stays on the lexicon. The heading is no longer said to read the verse 'the same way'.
- Church heading: it was quoted in three places and is now quoted once (40:9, 'will come and preach righteousness'); setting and 40:12 paraphrase it.
- 40:11: cut 'The countless troubles are set against countless mercies'. The verse 5 to verse 17 link ('thoughts' and 'thinketh') is now a plain observation of the King James wording, without 'rests on it'. The commentary read makes this link, but citing it here would pass the brief's limit of a third of the notes.
- 40:12: the opening no longer says in the chapter's voice that the psalm 'cannot be read of Jesus Christ in the same way from end to end'. The staged reading is labelled one reading throughout, the close says the Church's heading calls the psalm messianic and does not divide it, and the sentence about how 'this site' reads messianic psalms is replaced by a pointer to Ps. 22:1.
- 40:13: 'the differences are few' with three listed became 'small differences. Among them', since Ps. 70:2-3 differ in more words. The Psalm 35 sentence now quotes 35:4 as it is; 'Here, as there, he ... does nothing to the mockers himself' became 'In this psalm too the wishes are requests: he asks God to act'.
- christ: shortened to one paragraph that points to the second note on verse 6. The Heb. 10:10 quotation and the closing contrast were printed in both places.
- explore: cut 3 Ne. 9:19-20 and its row (no source read applies it to this psalm). Ps. 51:16-17 stays as a pointer within the book.
- Left as is: the commentary on Bible Hub is cited in 3 of 9 notes, exactly the brief's limit, each as one reading. No Latter-day Saint article, KnoWhy or Scripture Helps section treats this psalm, so the outside source stays where the question is real.
- Left as is: no note on the title (bare 'A Psalm of David'), and no note of its own on the curses of verses 14-15, which point to Ps. 35:4 and Ps. 137:8.

**Chapter 41**

- Section 1 plain (41:2-3): 'called blessed' put back to the King James 'be blessed', and 'turning sickness to health' cut; the row gives only the Hebrew 'change all his bed'.
- Thread: 'Those who come to him are the reverse of the blessed man of verse 1' reworded to what the older commentary says (the speaker is himself one of the weak and is treated the opposite way), with a new ledger row; 'says nothing true' became 'speaks emptily'; the last line now says verse 13 closes the first book.
- 41:1: cut the paragraph saying the psalm 'turns the word on its speaker' and the visitors 'are measured against the opening line' (the point now sits once, sourced, in the thread); 'That is why...' softened to 'the word can cover a man who is ill'; 'Amnon wasting away' became 'Amnon grown lean'.
- 41:4: cut 'My soul is the Hebrew way of saying me' (the row shows only a translation) and the writer's reading that he 'denies his enemies the verdict'; the note now states the confession, the lexicon's sense of 'integrity', and that the psalm does not say how the two fit.
- 41:8: cut the glosses 'some guilt that clings to him' and 'a fatal illness', the claim that the King James and a modern translation 'follow the second sense', and that the Greek 'took the noun as word'; the three renderings are now simply given. 'The whisper is a verdict' cut. 'The same Hebrew word' for rise up/raise me up became 'forms of the same Hebrew verb'.
- 41:9 (history): cut 'on settled good terms' and 'the blow comes from close by, from someone not being watched', neither of which is in a source.
- 41:9 (Witness of Christ): cut 'The supper acts the verse out', the paragraph on the omitted clause with John 13:11, and the sentences saying Peter's quoted lines come from two other psalms and 'reach beyond this verse' (no row names the psalms; the Church's footnote ties Acts 1:16 to this verse).
- 41:10: cut 'goes a step past the other psalms that ask for repayment', 'asks for the strength to do it', 'a ruler's justice and not a private man's revenge', and 'something smaller'; the note keeps the shared verb with Ps. 137:8, the one older reading, and the pointer to Ps. 137:8.
- 41:13: cut 'The change of voice shows it', 'the God of the whole people', 'past one man's sickness', and 'a word for those who hear a blessing as much as for the one who says it'; the last became 'a word of response', on a new Bible Dictionary row.
- Setting and 41:13: the same sentence on the five books was quoted in both; the setting now paraphrases and the quotation is printed once, in the note.
- Setting and christ: the Church's chapter heading was quoted in both; it is now quoted once, in the setting, and the christ row for it is removed.
- christ: cut the Heb. 4:15 contrast with 'I have sinned against thee' (the writer's own join, which the writer had flagged for the author) and the closing line 'The one the sick man asks to raise him up is the LORD', which hinted at a resurrection reading no source makes. 'Are not exclusive' softened to 'need not be seen as exclusive', as the source has it.
- Explore: 'The companion psalm' retitled 'Another friend who turned', since the pairing with Psalm 55 is one commentary's.
- Left as is: the older commentary stays in 2 of 7 notes (41:9, 41:10), each labelled a reading, plus the setting and now the thread; under the brief's third.
- Left as is: the Ahithophel reading beside the Church's Judas reading. It is given as one older reading of who David's friend was, not as an alternative to the Savior's use of the verse.
- Left as is: no note repeats a footnote or the heading; there is no Scripture Helps section on Psalm 41.

**Chapter 42**

- Tagline: "A temple singer" became "A man"; the title gives the psalm to the sons of Korah, but nothing says the speaker is one.
- Setting (finding 1): the Babylon proposal is now one of several ("other proposals differ, one putting him among those carried away to Babylon"), matching the four Delitzsch lists.
- Thread (finding 11): cut "The object of his remembering has changed from the festival to God" and "Memory turns in the middle"; the two verses are now quoted side by side without the join. "Asks God for nothing" became "has no petition", since verse 9 does question God and the sourced point is that the petition comes in Psalm 43. "Neither of the first two is answered in the psalm" stays as a plain observation of the text.
- 42:0 title (finding 2): left as it is. The brief places the full sons-of-Korah note here, and it goes past Scripture Helps at Psalm 49 (Num. 26:11, the Chronicles passages, the eleven psalms). Leaving authorship open agrees with Scripture Helps' "associated with"; Delitzsch's argument for authorship is not set beside it. Cut "Their psalms love the place" (a generalization from one psalm).
- 42:1: cut the pointer to Ps. 36:9 (no row, the writer's link).
- 42:2 (finding 3): "so the question is first of all when he will be at the temple again" replaced with what Skinner says of this verse: the psalmist longs to come to God in the temple.
- 42:4 (finding 4): cut "If the speaker did lead the throng, the memory suits a title that gives the psalm to a family of temple singers" (the note's own link).
- 42:5 structure (finding 5): cut "The shape is the message" and "The grief is not rebuked"; the close now gives Delitzsch's reading as one older reading ("overcome by the admonitory voice of his higher consciousness"), with a new ledger row I saw on the page.
- 42:5 Nephi (finding 6): "takes up" became "has this psalm's way of speaking"; cut the pairing of verse 9's "my rock" with 2 Ne. 4:30; the allusion is now "possible" and "not certain", as Hilton's "potential". Kept although footnote 11a gives 2 Ne. 4:28, because it adds 4:26 and 4:30 and says what kind of likeness it is.
- 42:6 Gethsemane (findings 7, 8): cut "Days earlier" (no row); cut the join from verse 9's "Why hast thou forgotten me?" to the cross; "the Greek Gospels give His words in the language of this psalm" narrowed to Matthew and Mark sharing the one word of the Greek refrain. John 12:27 stays only as the verse Delitzsch sets beside it, and is dropped from the parallels line that called it "the Greek word of the refrain".
- 42:7 (finding 10): cut "He does not say that trouble came while God looked away" and the lovingkindness/rock chain (the writer's reading, against verse 9); the note now says only that the spouts, waves and billows are all "thy". Cut the thirst-to-flood closing line, which repeated the thread.
- 42:11: cut the closing paragraph joining the "face" of verse 2 to the refrain (no source reads them together), and its ledger row.
- christ: cut the pairing of the psalmist's remembered feast with the feast of John 7 (the chapter's own). The first paragraph stays although it starts from footnote 2a, since a psalm with no scriptural reading of Christ gets a short section resting on the Church's sources (footnote and Guide to the Scriptures).
- explore (finding 9): cut "Thirst in a dry land" (footnote 2a alone, and Psalm 63:6 has no "song in the night"), "Living water" (repeats christ and footnote 2a; Jer. 2:13 had no source) and "Nephi's psalm" (repeats the note and footnote 11a); removed the explore ledger row.
- Delitzsch remains in 3 of 10 notes (refrain, Gethsemane, the place), each as an older reading, plus setting and thread; under the brief's third.

**Chapter 43**

- Setting and 43:1: the one-poem claim is no longer stated as fact. 'Does not begin a new poem so much as finish one' became 'reads as the close of the psalm before it', and 'that is why it has no title' was cut, since the Greek gives it a title and no source makes the causal claim. The note is retitled 'Psalms 42 and 43 as one poem'.
- 43:1: cut 'The third is the only one that opens with a request' (no row), and 'three stanzas of complaint' became 'three stanzas, each ending in the same refrain'.
- 43:1 ungodly: cut 'The charge, then, is narrower than irreligion' and the link to Ps. 42:8, which no source makes. The note is retitled 'The word behind ungodly'. The plain words and the thread now keep the King James 'an ungodly nation' in place of 'without loyalty'.
- 43:1 ungodly: the modern translation is now said to make 'man' plural, not to 'take it as a way of speaking of them all', which stated the translators' intent.
- 43:2: cut 'Being forgotten could be neglect. Being cast off is a decision.' Also cut the paragraph on the mourning verb as a word about darkness with 'The next verse asks for light': the lexicon's figurative sense is a mourner's neglected dress, and the dark-to-light pairing was the writer's.
- 43:3 light and truth: 'to find the singer and bring him home', 'best seen from two passages', 'On the strength of these parallels' and the closing paragraph ('does not ask for directions') cut. The lexicon sentence now says the verse is 'compared with' one where the Lord is a light to guide.
- 43:3 hill: the unsourced 'the mount where the Lord's house stood' became a pointer to Ps. 24:3, as the brief directs. Cut 'The journey ends in a person', the same-verb observation with 'one promise', and the 'your dwelling' translation sentence, which repeated footnote 3b.
- 43:5 soul: cut 'His soul is bent to the ground and will not be quiet' and 'nothing in the present is offered in support of it', both the writer's reading.
- 43:5 Gethsemane: the likeness is reduced to what the pages show, one shared Greek word said of 'my soul'. 'A likeness long noticed' and 'in the wording of this refrain' are cut, and the note now says the comparison is drawn at Ps. 42:5. I kept the note because a source read does apply the comparison and the note states its limit.
- christ: no longer opens with what the psalm lacks (the brief's habit to avoid). Cut 'No New Testament writer quotes Psalm 43, and no scripture reads it as a prophecy' and 'The God so named is Jehovah', which had no row; the psalm itself says 'God' throughout. 'The words He used' became 'the word He used'.
- Thread: the turn at verse 3 stays, reworded as what the text shows (the 'why' of verse 2 gives way to a request to be led). 'Stops asking why', 'in a harder form' and 'Nothing outward has changed. The singer is still where he was' are cut or softened.
- Explore: cut 'from the same distance' for Psalm 84 (no row) and the 'Mercy and truth sent out' bullet, which repeated the note on 43:3 and the parallels. The Matt. 26:38 parallel is reworded to 'share a Greek word'.
- Left as is: the older commentary read on Bible Hub in two of seven notes plus the thread, each labelled a reading. No Church, BYU or Scripture Central source treats these verses (no Scripture Helps chapter, no KnoWhy).
- Left as is: the 'Judge me' pointer to Ps. 26:1 although footnote 1a gives 'Vindicate'; it is a pointer of one clause, not a note.

- 46:6: 'Isaiah makes the comparison outright' is now 'Isaiah compares peoples and seas outright', the verse's plain words. - Thread: 'The nations do to the city what the sea did to the mountains' said more than the verses; now 'The nations rage and the kingdoms are moved in the words just used of the sea and the mountains'.
- christ: 'is Jehovah' (no row) removed from the first sentence; the KnoWhy quotation printed in both the Zenos note and christ is kept once, in the note; 'The same study suggests' is now 'It has also been suggested, tentatively'.
- explore: first bullet reworded to what each psalm has (kings hurry away in 48, broken weapons in 76; row requoted from Ps. 48:5); cut 'God with us' (repeated the note on verse 7, and Isaiah 8 has no raging nations) and 'Zion that shall come' (Moses 7:62-63 and D&C 101:18-21 unsupported; Moses 7:64 is already in the note) with their two rows.

Both are the Church's sources, each is given in its own note, and the fulfilment 'in effect' is reported as a reading; no source says how they fit, so the chapter does not.

**Chapter 48**

- 58:11 (finding 12): 'the onlookers are brought to confess' became 'those who had been misled are compelled to confess', which is what the commentary says.

**Chapter 60**

Added a ledger row for the heading.

- Setting: cut "so it comes from a people who knew that blessing" (the writer's inference). The heading "A messianic psalm" is now paraphrased there and quoted once, in the Christ section.
- Thread: cut "the longest verse in the psalm" and "The psalm addresses God directly only where the nations are in view" (no source, and verses 6-7 also have the ends of the earth in view). The ring description and the pronoun shifts stay as checkable in the text.
- Section 3 plain (67:6-7): returned to the King James future ("will give", "will fear"). The paraphrase had followed the NRSVUE's past tense and "revere" over the Church's edition; its two rows were removed.
- 67:1: cut "What the psalm puts in its place is verse 2" (the writer's join). The pointer to Ps. 4:6 stays, since Psalm 4 has that note, and now has a row quoting Ps. 4:6.
- 67:2 (Blessed so that the nations may know): "that is its whole point" became "One reading of the verse puts it as a rule". Cut the second Delitzsch quotation, which comes from his comment on verse 6, and removed its row.
- 67:2 (Saving health): the note listed the three references of footnote 2a, which the Gospel Library already shows. It is now a Language note on the Hebrew noun and the Greek and modern renderings. Mosiah 15:28 and D&C 133:37 were cut with their rows; Simeon (Luke 2:30-32) is quoted once, in the Christ section, as where the footnote points.
- 67:3: "every nation and not on Israel" became "the nations of the world and not on one people". Cut the closing paragraph ("That settles who is speaking to whom"), the writer's reading of a plural.
- 67:4: "the verb beside it is a shepherd's" became "means to lead" (the lexicon's sense). Cut "what Israel said of its own God", "there too it is good news" and "not for what He has done for Israel".
- 67:6: "other translations" became "the ancient Greek and a modern translation". Cut "the line is the psalm's evidence" and "God has done the small thing He promised, so He will do the great one". Cut the claim that the Church's footnotes "follow" the future reading; the note now says only what footnotes 6a and 6b give. The tenth article of faith is quoted once, in the Christ section.
- Christ, shining face: the step from 3 Nephi 19:25 to this psalm now rests on the Church's heading ("The Lord will cause His face to shine upon men") and on the Church's footnote at 3 Ne. 19:25, which gives Num. 6:25 (seen on the page this session). The KnoWhy's "fulfilled in a very literal sense" is given as a reading of that scene; "great high priest" was cut with its row.
- Christ, opening: "carry three of the psalm's lines to Jesus Christ" became "show where three of the psalm's lines lead".
- Christ, earth's renewal: the link from footnote 6b ("TG Earth, Renewal of") to the tenth article of faith stays. It rests on the article's own words ("the earth will be renewed"), not on the Topical Guide page, which would not load.
- Explore: cut the bullet on Psalms 96 and 98, which repeated the note on 67:4 and the parallels.
- Left as is, 67:0: "asks for stringed instruments" rests on the NRSVUE row, and Neginoth is explained at Psalm 4's title note.
- Left as is: Delitzsch in 2 of 7 notes, each as "one reading", plus setting, thread and explore; this is within the brief's limit.

**Chapter 68**

- Setting: cut 'stands entirely alone' and its row. In the commentary the phrase is part of an argument against the title's ascription to David, which the brief says no chapter takes up.
- Setting: 'The psalm is old, and it is hard' became 'The psalm is hard'; the early date stays only as what the Hebrew 'has been placed' in. 'Several of its words occur only here' became 'Two', the number the rows support.
- Setting and christ: the Church's 'messianic' chapter heading was mentioned in both; it now stands once, in christ, and the setting row was removed.
- Setting, 68:8 and parallels: 'almost word for word', 'The differences are small' and 'nearly the same Hebrew' reduced to 'share much of their wording', 'They differ too' and 'much the same Hebrew'; the note itself lists what differs.
- 68:8: cut the paragraph dating both poems to the earliest Hebrew, which repeated the setting, and removed the KnoWhy from the note's sources and its row. The echo of Deborah's song in verse 13 is now 'perhaps', as the lexicon marks it with a question mark.
- Thread: cut 'the description is not of a warrior' (verses 1-2 are warlike and no source says it), 'the summit, and the psalm turns there' (the source says only that the psalm divides in two after verse 18), and 'receive what He provides' for the woman of verse 12, who divides the spoil.
- Thread and 68:17: 'the verse joins the two mountains of the psalm' and the identification of the holy place with the hill of verse 16 were the writer's join; both now say only that verse 17 names Sinai and the holy place together.
- 68:1: cut the closing sentence that repeated the thread. Kept 'the same two words for the enemies' and added four Hebrew rows for it (Num. 10:35 and Ps. 68:1, read with hebrew.mjs).
- 68:4 (rider): cut the last paragraph, 'one rider in two places', a connection of the writer's own.
- 68:11: the reading now names Miriam and Deborah as the source does and marks that its list of deliverances is longer; cut the last paragraph, whose claim that the psalm says only that kings fled is contradicted by verse 14.
- 68:13: the lexicon's order restored: the Judges word is 'probably fire-places or ash-heaps', though most interpreters take it as 'sheepfolds'.
- 68:16: the lead is hedged ('probably watching with envy, not leaping'), since the sense comes from a verb found only here.
- 68:18: 'one word changed' became 'gave where the psalm has received'; 'without following the Greek version', said in the chapter's voice from the commentary, is replaced by the plain fact that the Greek has 'received'.
- 68:22: 'Both answers are old' became 'Two answers have been given'; the second reading's age has no source.
- 68:24: the lead now says the procession may be the worshippers' or God's own, as the lexicon does; the translation's verse 26 is said to be set in quotation marks, not to be 'the words the company sings'.
- 68:31: cut the tie to verse 29, 'looks through Egypt to the country beyond it', 'clear in every version', and the closing sentence that repeated the tagline; the note is now Cush and the uncertain word for 'Princes'.
- Left as is, 68:20 and 68:6: thin (lexicon and translations) but each answers a question the King James wording raises and neither repeats a Church footnote.
- Left as is, 68:1, 68:8, 68:18: the Church's footnotes give the references, but each note goes past them (the Hebrew compared, what is changed, Paul's 'gave' for 'received').
- Left as is, setting: the two occasions stay as two proposals although the commentary prefers the second; the chapter claims no more than that both have been proposed.
- Left as is, christ: its second paragraph rests on what the psalm says of the Lord under the name JAH, as the brief directs for a psalm's christ section; no typology added.
- Left as is: Delitzsch in 4 of 14 notes (68:11, 13, 18, 22), under the brief's third, each labelled a reading. No Scripture Helps chapter covers Psalm 68, so there was no endnote to follow.

**Chapter 69**

- Setting and 69:35: 'for that reason' and 'which is why' cut, since Scripture Helps gives no reason for the later dating. The link between the ending and the exile is now given as one older reading, with two new Delitzsch rows.
- 69:35: cut the 'rebuild' rendering (footnote 35b already has it), the unsourced 'Read so, the ending has in view a people who have lost their land', and the Scripture Helps sentence on the zeal of verse 9, which gave only one of its two readings and sits beside the verse in the Gospel Library. The dating itself is now paraphrased, not quoted.
- Thread: 'out of zeal for God's house' reworded to what verses 9–11 say in order; 'each matched to a wrong' and 'not the speaker's innocence' cut.
- 69:1: cut 'its own plea later shows what the water stands for', 'the plea comes close' and 'What he fears from the water is death'. The observation that verse 14 sets the haters beside the waters stays, as something the reader can check in the line.
- 69:4: cut both Delitzsch sentences ('more probable' that John quotes this psalm, and the proverbial gloss). Neither was needed, and cutting them keeps Delitzsch to 4 of 14 notes now that 69:35 cites him.
- 69:5: cut the sentence joining Delitzsch's 'typically prophetic' to the Church's heading, a join no source makes; 'On that view' became 'On that reading'.
- 69:9: 'The verses around it show what the zeal looked like' reworded to a plain statement of verses 10–11. The paragraph counting the Hebrew word for reproach is cut because the thread already says it.
- 69:21: cut 'In the psalm the drink is an insult. At the cross, on that account, the first was meant kindly', the writer's own contrast, which the writer had flagged.
- 69:22: 'never as the Savior's' cut (unsourced for the whole New Testament). The closing paragraph, Delitzsch's opinion that the curses 'are not appropriate to the lips of the suffering Saviour', is cut as an outside view the reader's question does not need beside a psalm the Church heads as messianic.
- 69:26: cut 'he has confessed sins of his own (verse 5)' and 'they were wrong about the reason'; the contrast now rests on the plain words of Isaiah 53:4–5.
- 69:28: 'an image that goes back to Moses' reworded to 'the image of Moses's prayer after the golden calf'; cut 'The psalmist asks God to do what He there said He does' and the verse 27 equivalence.
- christ: the comparison with the end of Psalm 22 is kept as one that 'has been compared', with a new Delitzsch row ('We are at once reminded of Psalm 22:27'); the 'seed' sentence, the writer's own, is cut.
- explore: the D&C 122:7–8 bullet is cut (no source joins it to this psalm); the Psalm 40:2 bullet is retitled 'The miry clay' and no longer says 'the same mire'.
- 69:31: the remark that shir (song) and shor (ox) sound alike stays, as a fact about the two words that claims no intent.
- 69:4 (finding 4): the pointer to Psalm 35:19 stays; that note says John does not say which psalm the Lord had in mind, and this note no longer says otherwise.
- 69:0, 69:20, 69:21, 69:25 (finding 15): each goes beyond the footnotes and Scripture Helps (the pointer to Psalm 45:1; Elder Talmage, not the Holland passage Scripture Helps prints; the two drinks and John 19:28; the splice with Psalm 109:8), so all stay.

**Chapter 70**

- Setting (finding 1): 'David's psalm was ... modified' became 'the original psalm was ... modified', since Delitzsch makes David's authorship of the original a condition; his judgment that David did not write this form stays out (the brief: no chapter argues for or against a title). Ledger claim reworded to match.
- Setting (finding 2): 'their closing self-description' became 'this psalm's last verse and the line "But I am poor and sorrowful" (69:29)', since 69:29 is not the close of Psalm 69.
- Setting: cut 'The Church's edition heads the psalm with the saying of verse 4' and its row; it only repeated the Gospel Library chapter heading.
- Thread: 'he does not count himself among the glad' became 'he says of himself that he is "poor and needy"' (the writer's inference).
- 70:0 title note: cut the lexicon's 'commemorate, praise' and the NRSVUE 'memorial offering', which the Psalm 38 title note already gives; the note now points there and keeps only the Greek title's own wording. NRSVUE row and key removed from this note.
- 70:2 (finding 4): cut 'The pairing shows what the speaker thinks is at stake. He does not ask that his friends be given the last word ... or that anyone say he was right' (no source).
- 70:3 (finding 3): cut 'The mockers are not threatening him. They are enjoying what has happened to him'; 'ask for the same thing twice: that those who came after him retreat' became 'verse 3, like verse 2, asks that they be turned back' (the verses' own words).
- 70:5 (finding 5): cut 'the words ... say nothing of his rank' (no source, and BDB lists the words of a king); the opening sentence now says only what BDB supports ('afflicted and needs God's deliverance') and no longer 'not a statement about money'.
- 70:5 (finding 6): 'put it a third time as a negative' became 'add its negative' (a different Hebrew verb); cut the closing paragraph 'things already true of the God who has not yet come' (unsourced, and the thread already quotes the words).
- 70:5: cut the Delitzsch sentence 'the hope is turned into petition' and its row; the two verses quoted show it, and the brief makes Delitzsch a last resort. Delitzsch now stands only in setting, as 'one older reading'.
- christ (finding 8): the paragraph applying 'He' to verse 4's words addressed to God (Elohim) was replaced by what verses 1 and 5 say beside the name LORD ('to help me'; 'thou art my help and my deliverer').
- christ (finding 7): kept 'Hebrews quotes verses 6-8 of that psalm and not this plea' and added a ledger row quoting Ps. 40:6 so the match is on record.
- explore: removed Ps. 141:1, the one cross-reference the Church's footnote on 70:5 already gives.

**Chapter 71**

- Setting (findings 1, 3): cut the sentence giving one older commentary's proposal that Jeremiah wrote the psalm, with its two ledger rows. It stood beside the Church's heading, which speaks of David, and the reader's question does not need it. The setting now says the Greek title and the Church's heading both speak of David and that the psalm names no one.
- Setting and explore (finding 1): removed 'Book Two' and 'closes the book', which had no ledger row. The setting now says only that the psalm stands between Psalm 70 (David's by its title) and the psalm for Solomon.
- Setting (finding 2): cut 'whom the LORD commends' (the commendation is not at Jer. 35:6) and 'so that title remembers the psalm as one sung by the first exiles' (the writer's inference). The Rechabites are now identified from the words of Jer. 35:6, quoted.
- Thread, turn at verse 14 (finding 4): left as written. The brief asks the thread to say where the psalm turns, and the reader can check it in the text ('But I will hope continually').
- 71:1 (finding 5): left as written. The borrowings are supported, the reason for them is labelled 'one older reading', and the enemies paragraph only points to Ps. 137:8.
- 71:3: 'either the Hebrew line has been changed on purpose or it has suffered in copying' (the writer's inference) became a statement that translators divide between the Hebrew and the Greek.
- 71:6 (finding 6): cut 'before he could have chosen to trust anyone' and 'That is the ground of the plea' (the writer's reading); the close now says only what verses 5, 6 and 9 say. The Qumran manuscript in the translation's note stays unmentioned, since the abbreviation was not read.
- 71:7 (finding 7): cut 'His enemies read him the second way. In verse 11...' and 'his answer to any reading of his life', which joined verses 7 and 11 on the writer's own authority. The two senses of the word stay, since the lexicon lists Deut. 28:46 under it.
- 71:9 (finding 8): kept as a short Latter-day Prophets note. 'Quoted this verse as a charge to the families' became 'repeated this verse when he turned to the families', which is what the talk does. The note already says the psalm's own plea is to God.
- 71:11: 'Here he never does' was unclear, since he uses the verb twice in his pleas; it now reads 'he never says that God has forsaken him'. The note rests on the Hebrew and the text only, which the standard allows for a repeated word.
- 71:16 (finding 12): 'An earlier psalm' became 'Another psalm' (no source dates Psalm 40).
- 71:18 (finding 9): the Isaiah 46:3-4 comparison is kept as shared words. Both Hebrew nouns are confirmed in both verses, a modern translation cross-references verse 6 to Isa. 46:3, and the note says no source claims that either passage has the other in view. 'Shows that he means the rising one' was softened to 'suggests'.
- 71:20 (finding 10): cut 'and does not discuss this one further' (the checker could not verify it), and 'possible allusions' became 'allusions', matching the study's own 'textual allusions'.
- 71:22, christ and explore (finding 11): 'Nephi, who uses it often' became 'The title is frequent in the Book of Mormon', as the Bible Dictionary says. The 2 Ne. 25:29 quotation now stands once, in the note; christ points to it; the note's paragraph that repeated christ and the duplicate explore bullet were cut.
- christ, second paragraph: left as written. The brief allows a short christ section resting on what the psalm says of the Lord, and it is worded as a reading ('Read with that name').

**Chapter 72**

- Setting: "verse 20 is a line about the collection" was stated as fact; it now says only what verse 20 says and points to the note.
- 72:0 (title): cut Delitzsch's argument that the preposition "cannot have any other meaning" than authorship, with its two rows. It argued against the Church heading ("David speaks of Solomon") and the brief says no chapter argues a title. The note keeps the Hebrew, the translations, the heading and President Holland.
- 72:1: the comparison with Solomon's request in 1 Kgs. 3:9 was the writer's own. It is now cited to the Cambridge Bible ("the echo of God's offer to Solomon") as one older reading; "that night" (no row) became "that prayer".
- 72:2: cut "manifestly optatives" (Delitzsch says it only of verses 2-4) and "asks and does not foretell". The note rests on the jussives and the modern translation and says "the psalm is first a prayer". "Solomon's reign did not answer a prayer this large" became "did not reach the ends of the earth", since Delitzsch says the psalm was fulfilled in Solomon except for universal dominion.
- 72:2: left the sentence that verse 20 calls what precedes it "prayers"; it holds whether the line closes this psalm or a collection.
- 72:3: cut "the crop depends on the king's justice", Isa. 32:17 with its row, "a just king and a fruitful land go together" and "The word suits this king" (the writer's own).
- 72:5: the Delitzsch quotation is replaced by the plain observation that God is addressed and the king is in the third person. Church footnote 5a (Ps. 89:36) hangs on "endure" and does not make "thee" the king, so the note stands.
- 72:6: cut the paragraph on David's last words and "the psalm has all five" with two rows; "which is why" the Greek has "fleece" became a plain "and". 2 Sam. 23:3-4 stays in parallels, now with a Cambridge row ("may have been suggested by").
- 72:8: Ex. 23:31 kept, now as one older reading (Cambridge: "a poetical generalisation of the promise to Israel"). Cut "That is two seas"; the note now says the psalm does not say which seas and the phrase may be general. "Zechariah repeats" became "has".
- 72:10: cut "the far ends of the world Israel knew" and "the western end of the Mediterranean" (no row). Added the Bible Dictionary's caution that "ships of Tarshish" came to mean a kind of ship. The Isaiah 60:6 sentence now quotes the verse.
- 72:12: cut Deut. 17:17 and the implied chain that Solomon broke it. The note keeps 1 Kgs. 4:25, 1 Kgs. 12:4 and Delitzsch's "the end of his reign was not like the beginning".
- 72:14: cut "duty", "The king takes the place of the kinsman he lacks" and "says why the king redeems it" (the writer's own reading).
- 72:15: "the people's prayer for their king" (true only if Solomon wrote the psalm) became "prayer made for the king". Cut the closing "The blessing passes from the king to the One the first verse asked".
- 72:16: retitled "A handful of corn, or plenty of grain?" and the opening hedged to "probably", because the lexicon marks the word doubtful.
- 72:17: the Abrahamic link is kept, now cited to the Cambridge Bible ("an allusion to the promises to Abraham and Isaac"). The same-verb claim is narrowed to Gen. 22:18, with rows for its Hebrew and for the Greek of Gen. 12:3. Cut "its wish is that the blessing ... reach the nations through this king". Kept Gal. 3:16, worded as Paul's statement about the Genesis promise.
- 72:18: cut "The blessing answers the psalm it follows" and "the last wish is larger". Num. 14:21 stays, though it is Church footnote 19a, because the note quotes it and goes further (the divine name, the pointer to Ps. 150:6).
- 72:20: retitled "The prayers of David are ended". President Holland now comes first, with "the text would seem to indicate" restored; the collection reading follows as one older reading. Job 31:40 is now cited to Delitzsch, who makes the comparison.
- christ: cut "and Solomon's had both" (no row), Luke 1:32-33 and Rev. 11:15 with their rows (the writer's own, as cut in Psalms 20 and 82). Matt. 12:42 stays, with a pointer to the Zechariah and Matthew link in note 72:8.
- Parallels: the 1 Kgs. 3:9, 2 Sam. 23:3-4 and Gen. 22:18 entries are reworded as readings and each given a Cambridge row.
- Explore: left as it is. The Isa. 11 and Ps. 89 lines say only what those passages say, and footnote 5a links Ps. 89:36.
- Sources: the proposed file now has two entries. bd-solomon is unchanged. The page title seen was "Psalm 72 Cambridge Bible for Schools and Colleges"; it names no author, so the entry gives none.

**Chapter 73**

- 73:10 (findings 1, 2): cut 'the readings do agree that the wicked draw a crowd' (the Greek says no such thing) and retitled the note 'A verse the versions read differently' (ledger rows retitled). The last paragraph now gives verse 11's speakers in Delitzsch's words, with a new row; 'Success persuades' and the sentence after it are cut.
- 73:15 (findings 3, 4): cut 'From here on the psalm is prayer' (verses 16-17 are narrative), 'He will not unsettle the others with a doubt he cannot resolve', and 'which is this psalm's verses 3-12 in one line'. The Malachi 3:14 link stays: the site's Malachi chapter sources it (Keil, 'cf. Psalm 73:13') and the note points there.
- 73:17 (findings 5, 6): cut the sentence that the sanctuary was where Asaph worked, with its Bible Dictionary row. Cut Delitzsch's this-life reading of the wicked's end, which stood beside the Church's chapter heading; the heading's 'hereafter' is now the note's answer, with one word quoted.
- 73:20 (finding 7): cut 'The psalm does not mean that God sleeps', 'after long patience', 'He has been looking on', and the sentence about envying what will not survive the morning; none had a source. Psalm 78:65 is now given as a comparison. I left the NRSVUE's marking of 'Lord' as a correction unmentioned; the note does not need it.
- 73:23 (findings 8, 9): the verse 2 to verse 23 link is now only the two verses' plain words; 'did not fall because he was being held' and 'At his worst he was with God' are cut. 'The verse shows' became conditional, as Calabro has it; 'Latter-day Saint scholars have argued' became 'Several scholars have proposed', as the KnoWhy has it. The pointer to 63:4 now says 'See also', since I did not read that note to confirm it is the fuller one.
- 73:26 (finding 5): kept the Levite reading, following the brief's rule that a title is the setting the psalm asks to be read in, but reworded it to 'Read as its title asks'. Cut the paragraph that verse 26 answers verse 4 (the writer's gloss) and kept only that 'strength' is the Hebrew 'rock'.
- 73:28 (finding 10): cut the sentences on measuring God's goodness by health and wealth and on the speaker now having something to tell God's children. The same-word observations for 'good' and 'declare'/'speak' stay.
- christ (finding 11): cut D&C 88:63, the writer's own juxtaposition, with its row. The opening sentence stays, following the brief's rule for a psalm no source reads of Christ.
- Finding 12 confirmed the ketiv and qere of 73:10; nothing to change.
- Setting: cut the Calabro quotation about 'wisdom literature' (a term no source read defines) and its row. The Jeremiah 12 and Job parallels now rest on a Delitzsch row that names them.
- Thread: cut 'The slipping he feared for himself in verse 2 belongs, he now sees, to them'; the link is in the English only, and the Hebrew words differ.
- 73:3: cut 'He does not say the prophets are wrong'. 73:4: 'the same letters with a space moved' became 'the same consonants, divided into two words'. 73:24: '(the commentary counts verses by the Hebrew)' became '(49:15 in the King James numbering)'. Section 3 plain: verse 14 restored to 'chastened every morning'.
- Delitzsch stays in 3 of 11 notes (10, 17, 24), under the brief's third; no Student Manual is cited.

**Chapter 74**

- Setting (finding 3): dropped the unattributed Delitzsch quotation about Psalm 79 and the quoted Church chapter heading; Psalm 79 'has been read as coming from the same circumstances', and Lamentations 'sounds like some of' the psalm's lines, so setting no longer says more than the note on verse 9.
- Thread (finding 1): 'A lament commonly closes with a vow of praise' became 'A vow of praise is one of the usual parts of a lament', which is all KnoWhy #438 says.
- Thread (finding 2): cut 'What has changed is where it is looking' (the writer's reading); the thread now says the psalm reports no change in the ruins and that its subject changes.
- 74:0 (quoting Church material sparingly): the three quotations from the Bible Dictionary and Scripture Helps are now paraphrased; the rows stay as evidence.
- 74:2 (finding 4): retitled 'The words of the song at the sea' and reworded to shared wording only. No source says the psalm draws on Exodus 15, and the brief forbids saying who borrowed from whom; ledger rows retitled to match.
- 74:4 (findings 5, 14): the pairing of 'their signs' and 'our signs' is now given as one older reading from the first sentence; the lexicon is no longer announced.
- 74:5 (finding 6): left as written; the King James's contrast is the verses' own 'But now'.
- 74:7 (findings 7, 8, 14): cut 'and left the house standing' (an inference not on the page); 'one scholar's judgment and not a settled result' became 'one older commentator's judgment, and a hesitant one', which his own words support; the 'older commentary... the same commentary' wording became 'the older reading'.
- 74:8 (finding 9): cut 'The Hebrew does not say what kind of building they were' (no row, and the lexicon and Delitzsch both say synagogues); the three renderings are given without announcing the footnote or lexicon; the closing sentence is now only a pointer to the note on verse 7.
- 74:9 (findings 10, 14): 'Neither is settled' replaced by what the source does (it prefers the later date); 'one older commentary observes' removed; 'mourns the same thing' became 'says something close', since the note itself says the match is imperfect.
- 74:11 (finding 11): cut the Exodus 15:6 link (the writer's own) and its row; the NRSVUE footnote now 'marks the rendering as a correction of the Hebrew', not 'a difficult Hebrew clause'; added a row for Lam. 2:3, read on the Church's page this session.
- 74:14 (findings 12, 14): opening now states the Bible Dictionary's and lexicon's reading (a nation, Egypt); cut 'the sea monster of ancient poetry', 'the order of the lines agrees' and the Ezekiel 29:3 comparison (no source applies it here); Isaiah 51:9-10 kept because the Church's footnote 13a makes the link; the creation reading is labelled 'One reading' and the KnoWhy's chaos-into-order line is said of the Old Testament and the Babylonian story, as the KnoWhy says it, not of this psalm alone; cut the lexicon's 'wild beast' gloss and its row, since the lexicon emends the text at this verse.
- 74:19 (finding 13): 'Israel is God's turtledove' became 'The congregation calls itself God's turtledove', which the verse's parallel line shows; the Greek variant is stated as what the Greek has, not as what the translators read.
- 74:20 (findings 2, 15): cut the Lamentations 1:18 comparison (the writer's own, no row) and 'Its arguments all belong to God's side of the bond'; kept the Psalm 79:8 contrast as an observation of the two texts.
- christ (finding 16): kept 1 Cor. 10:1-4, which the Guide to the Scriptures gives for Christ as Jehovah and which the Church's footnote at verse 15 reaches through Ex. 17:6; cut 'The monster has a later history in scripture'; the 2 Nephi 9:10 paragraph stays as KnoWhy #34's reading of the Old Testament sea monsters generally, Leviathan among them.
- explore (finding 17 and overlaps): cut the D&C 121 bullet and its row (no source ties it to this psalm; the same decision as at Psalms 6 and 10); trimmed the Psalm 79 bullet so it does not repeat the note on verse 20; removed Isaiah 27:1 and 51:9-11 from 'Leviathan elsewhere' because the note already gives them.
- Limits: Delitzsch stays in 3 of 12 notes (74:4, 74:7, 74:9), each labelled a reading; no Student Manual; the Church's heading is cited once, in the note on verse 7.

**Chapter 75**

- 75:8 Witness of Christ (C; writer's flag): kept, now sourced. The NT Student Manual at Mark 14:36 applies Ps. 75:8 to the Savior's cup and joins it to 3 Ne. 11:11 and D&C 19:18; two rows added. The opening no longer says what the psalm lacks. It is the chapter's only Student Manual note and also rests on KnoWhy #341.
- 75:8 cup of judgment (D, A9, A10): cut the Mosiah 3:26 / Alma 40:26 paragraph (the Church's footnote 8a, and repeated in the Christ note) and 'The point is completeness...'; 'more likely foams' became 'is taken as', since the lexicon does not rank the two. The Alma row moved to the Christ note; an Isa. 51:17 row replaced the Mosiah row.
- setting (A1, A2): 'draws on an older song' became 'stands close to'; 'at Shiloh' cut; the Assyrian date is now 'a probability and no more, about even with' the Maccabean one, with a new row; 'Its place in the book says more' cut. The setting is now about 230 words.
- 75:7 (A1, A11, B): retitled 'Hannah's song beside the psalm' (rows retitled); 'Hannah's in substance' became 'nearly Hannah's'; cut 'The difference is one of scale', 'after the birth of her son' and 'later ... of empires'; the close now sets the verses side by side.
- thread (A3, A4): the three-voice scheme is marked as the reading followed; 'That can only be God' cut; the writer's own 'the line can as easily be God's closing word' cut, and the thread points to the note on verse 2. One thread row removed.
- 75:2 (A6, A7): cut 'sounds like a king who promises to rule justly' and 'That is how congregation came into this verse' (now: the King James gives the noun the same English word here); cut 'a line the Hebrew leaves open'; 'In one older commentary's words' removed so no source is announced; the opening says the 'I' 'is read as' God.
- 75:3 (A8, D): cut 'less an earthquake than a human world losing its nerve', 'keeping the earth from collapse meanwhile', and the closing pointer to Hannah's pillars (footnote 3a; the verse 7 note has it).

- 75:1 (B): cut 'the congregation gives thanks because God is at hand, and in the next verse He speaks' (the writer's own). The word-for-word sentence stays; it has an oshb-wlc row.
- 75:4 (B): 'A horn stands for strength' became a pointer to Ps. 18:2, where the horn is explained; cut 'because the wicked are lifting their own' and the closing sentence that repeated the thread.

- explore (A13): cut 'Who sets up kings' (Dan. 2:20-22 is not about Babylon's king, Dan. 4:17 had no row, Dan. 2:21 is in the note) and 'The prayer this psalm answers' (the setting already has Ps. 74:22).
- Left as is: the 75:0 title note (short, gives the meaning and points to Ps. 57:1, 8:1 and 50:1 as the brief requires); the missing-north reading in 75:6 (labelled one older reading and an inference); 'Nothing in the text marks a change of speaker' and Mary's song in explore (observations of the text, the second with a row).

Setting is 196 words.
- Thread (finding 2): the four-part division is now worded as the way the chapter reads the psalm ('Read in four parts of three verses each'), not as a fact about the psalm. - Section 4 plain (finding 17): now follows the King James ('what remains of wrath You will restrain') and says the Hebrew is uncertain, instead of printing the rendering the lexicon calls 'very doubtful' as the verse's sense; 'the kings of the earth fear Him' became 'He is fearsome to the kings of the earth'.

- 76:5 'The sleepers' (findings 11, 12): 'an ancient tradition' became 'the ancient Greek title'; cut 'Kings reports no battle, and neither does the psalm' and the Nahum 3:18 sentence, a connection no source read makes for this verse (3 rows removed).
