## Reader questions no source answered

*(`scripts/batch-notes.mjs` appends them here.)*
- **1:** Psalm 1:3, "whatsoever he doeth shall prosper": how does this stand beside the prosperity of the wicked that Jeremiah 12:1 and Psalm 73 complain of? No Church, BYU or Scripture Central source read applies those passages to this verse. The chapter answers only from Delitzsch's comment on verse 5, as one reading; Does Jeremiah 17:5-8 draw on the psalm, or the psalm on Jeremiah? (The writer's question; no Latter-day Saint source read speaks to it.).
- **2:** Psalm 2:7: what day is 'this day'? The only source read that answers it is Delitzsch (the day of the king's anointing), and a third Delitzsch note would pass the brief's limit; KnoWhy #865 and Larsen do not say. The writer's unsourced inference was cut; Has a latter-day prophet taught from Psalm 2? The writer's one search found no conference talk; there is no Latter-day Prophets note; Psalm 2:12: who is 'him' in 'put their trust in him', the Son or the LORD? Delitzsch says the LORD; no Church or Latter-day Saint source read says either, so the chapter leaves it in the verse's words.
- **3:** Does a latter-day prophet or Restoration scripture take up Psalm 3? The writer found none (no Joseph Smith Translation footnote, no KnoWhy, no talk); I did not search further; Why is there no selah after verse 6? Delitzsch has an explanation (the quiet tone of the line), but it is his alone, so the chapter leaves it out.
- **8:** What the Restoration adds to 'What is man?' (8:4): Moses 1:8-10, 37-39 is the obvious companion, but no source read joins it to this psalm, so the pointer was cut. Scripture Helps endnotes 20-21 ('The Origin of Man', 'Becoming Like God') are readable and were not used; the note on 8:5 gives only the one-sentence teaching that God's children can become like Him; Whether a latter-day prophet has taught from Psalm 8 beyond quoting it (8:3-5): none found by the writer; Why infants, of all people, are set against 'the enemy and the avenger' (8:2): answered only by one older commentary's reading; no Church, BYU or Scripture Central source found on it.
- **22:** What in David's life the psalm answers to: the title gives no occasion and no source read offers one; What the last clause of verse 29 ('none can keep alive his own soul') means in Hebrew: only translation footnotes were read, so the note says only that translations differ; Why one man's deliverance reaches all nations, the dead and the unborn (22:27-31): the text shows the widening; the reason is given only as readings (Hopkin, Elder McConkie).
- **23:** Psalm 23:5: why the picture changes from a flock to a feast, and what it meant to eat at a host's table before one's enemies (whether a guest was under the host's protection). No source read explains it; the note only describes the change; Psalm 23:6: whether 'for ever' in the house of the Lord is a promise of eternal life. The note gives the Hebrew ('length of days'); no Church or scholarly source read carries the line further; Psalm 23: what a latter-day prophet has taught from the psalm. The writer found no conference talk on it, so there is no Latter-day Prophets note; Psalm 23:5: whether a host anointing a guest's head was a known custom. The Luke 7:46 illustration was cut because no source read applies it to this verse.
- **24:** Psalm 24:7-10: who speaks the call to the gates and who asks 'Who is this King of glory?' No Latter-day Saint source read assigns the voices; Psalm 24:1-2: why a psalm about the temple hill opens with the Creation, beyond 'owner because Creator'. Parry notes the creation scene is prominent but does not say why, and no readable source was found for the temple as the mountain rising from the waters.
- **51:** 51:16: why 'else would I give it' sounds as if no offering were open to David (whether the law's having no sacrifice for murder or adultery lies behind it); no source read says; 51:5: what the parallelism of 'shapen in iniquity ... in sin did my mother conceive me' is doing; the writer found nothing a source carried beyond Scripture Helps; 51:18-19: no Latter-day Saint source was found on whether the last two verses are David's or a later addition; the note rests on Delitzsch alone.
- **82:** When Psalm 82 was written: no source read dates it; Which occasion, if any, the death sentence of verse 7 refers to: only one scholar's suggestion was found; Whether any source ties 'thou shalt inherit all nations' (82:8) directly to Deuteronomy 32:8-9: the studies read apply Deut. 32:8 to the psalm's council but not to verse 8, so the note gives only the shared words.
- **110:** Did a latter-day prophet or Joseph Smith teach from Psalm 110?
